## Amoebic crawler

Amoebic crawlers were a gelatinous species of ooze from the Far Realm.

Description
Amoebic crawlers resembled large, translucent worms with two tentacles emerging from the "head." They derived from the fluid of the Amoebic Sea itself, and exuded a palpable dread as they crawled.

Personality
Amoebic crawlers were mindless creatures, but were voracious hunters.

Abilities
Amoebic crawlers were difficult to spot, owing to their translucent color, and many an adventurer had accidentally walked into their acidic bodies. However, unlike a black pudding, the amoebic crawler could not corrode stone or metal, but only organic materials. Additionally, the crawler's ties to the Far Realm led them to exhibit some form of touch-based telepathy, allowing them to fill their victims with horrifying visions of their home plane and causing the victim intense psychic pain and nausea.

Combat
Amoebic crawlers would propel themselves toward a creature and lash out with their two lithe, acidic tentacles. If it managed to hit the creature, it could restrain its foe and begin an external digestion process, stripping flesh from bones in plain sight. The bodies of their victims were then incorporated into their mass.

Relationships
Amoebic crawlers were often used by the kaorti, who used these oozes in their quest to reform the Material Plane.

History
When the Imaskari wizards called the Quin returned from their millenia long sojourn in the Far Realm, they returned transformed into the Kaorti. With them they brought other creatures of that alien place, including amoebic crawlers. Other amoebic crawlers were later brought in through portals back to the Far Realm created deep in the Kaorti lairs.

References

## Amaunator's Legacy

Amaunator's Legacy was a unique enchanted bastard sword created by Pouldinus, one of the Archwizards of Netheril.

Description
This sword was an extremely old shiny brass weapon.

Powers
Amaunator's Legacy was a weapon with a powerful +3 enchantment and its magics were deadly to the undead. When the sword was used against the undead, it burned and sizzled them with magical flames. There was also a small chance of the sword unleashing the Beltyn's burning blood spell at any creature it stricken. Additionally, the sword allowed the wielder to cast the sunray spell once per day at the range of .

History
Amaunator's Legacy was created more than 3,000 years before Dale Reckoning by Pouldinus to be gifted to his friend, a stern warrior who venerated Amaunator, the Keeper of the Eternal Sun. Kalamnin used the sword to help follow the deity's edicts exactly. Amaunator's Legacy remained in Kalamnin's hand throughout his travels and left only when the warrior was slain in battle against several dozens of nycadaemons. Amaunator's Legacy was claimed by the fiends' summoner, the Sorcerer named Ylmandi. The summoner's family owned the bastard sword until the fall of Netheril during Karsus's Folly.

The sword reappeared once again in the  in the hands of a halfling priest of Waukeen of the Golden Lodge in the town of Lonelywood in Icewind Dale.

## Amantha Indesm

One early spring afternoon in the, Amantha Indesm was among the guests at Haelithtorntowers for a meeting with Joysil Ambrur. When she was about to leave, she embraced her host before departing. A fellow guest, Noumea Cardellith, noticed that she'd cast a spell on Joysil at that point, and warned their host once Amantha was out of earshot. Joysil explained that Amantha did that every time, and then consented to have the spell broken.

## Ambere Dunn

Ambere Dunn was a woman who lived in the town of Lonelywood in Icewind Dale in the late 13 century DR. She was the unhappy and heavy-drinking wife to Tybald Dunn and mother to Hailee Dunn.

Description
Ambere was a human woman with a light skin tone and long golden hair.

History
Tybald Dunn and Ambere married a number of years before somewhere south of Icewind Dale. Eventually, the couple moved to the frontier town of Lonelywood, as Tybald promised his wife a "bright new future." However, she and Tybald had a hellion of a daughter. Ambere spent her days drowning her misery at a frozen hell of a home, dead-end husband, and unruly daughter in drinks at The Whistling Gallows inn. She was sleeping around, throwing herself at visitors and caravaners who passed through the town in hopes that one of them would take her away from this life. Despite being bedded by several men who promised her an escape, Ambere was left behind by every single one of them.

When a barbarian army threatened the Ten Towns in the, a group known as the Heroes of Icewind Dale arrived in Lonelywood to deal with the danger. By some accounts, the heroes met the Dunn family and subsequently managed to convince Ambere to reconsider her plans of escape and to rethink her priorities, putting effort into her family. It was also possible that the heroes saw Ambere and her drinking habits as detriments for her family and convinced her to flee Lonelywood on her own and start a new life somewhere else, to Tybald's dismay.

## Ambidexterity

Ambidexterity was the mundane ability or trait to proficiently use both hands in any given task, having equal amounts of coordination and skill with either, rather than having a primary left or right hand. Those who possessed the trait were referred to as ambidextrous.

Some considered it possible for the trait to be "learned" later in life through regularly training someone to use their less dominant hand. Possession of this trait was considered by many to be essential for fighting in a dual-weapon style, such as the drow martial art draa velve.

Magic
Ambidexterity could be artificially obtained by certain magic items, such as wearing the gloves of the balanced hands, the Nightthief's Nimbleknife, or wielding both of the silver sickles of Soranth. However, when these magic items ceased to be worn or wielded, the user would cease to be ambidextrous.

Society
Ambidexterity was most commonly exhibited among rogues and warriors. Some particular types of rogues or warriors known for exhibiting this trait included acrobats, assassins, duelists, sharpshooters, and swashbucklers, and weapon masters. And many of the fighter-rogue slavers of the Xanathar's Thieves' Guild were known to be ambidextrous.

Other types of adventurers in the Realms that were commonly seen to exhibit this trait included blades, bladesingers, and breachgnomes.

Beyond humans and demihumans, for whom ambidexterity was not an innate trait to all members of a given race and could even be rather rare, there were some creatures in the Realms for whom all members were naturally ambidextrous. These included alaghi, chitines, desmodus, drider vampires, greater doppelgangers, hadozee, malenti, and thri-kreen. For some races ambidexterity was quite common, but not an absolute certainty, such as being exhibited by most tieflings and wang-liang. Some undead were gifted with this trait regardless of whether or not the person they were in life was ambidextrous, such as deathbringers.

History
When Nymorah, an annual peace festival and tournament in Ravens Bluff that was enacted by Lord Mayor Sirrus Melandor, was first held one of the teams that competed was Pure and Passionate, a group of Tormtar women from Tantras. Among their numbers were three half-elf rangers that were all ambidextrous.

Known Ambidextrous People

## Amulet of Valindra's favor

Amulet of Valindra's favor were enchanted amulets and cloak clasps that were created to be boons for Valindra Shadowmantle's most loyal servants in the late 15 century DR.

Powers
The wearers of the amulets received boosts to their vitality, ability to land lucky devastating strikes, accuracy, and constitution. When an amulet of Valindra's favor was worn together with a belt of Valindra's guard and a shard of Valindra's crown, the amulet greatly fortified the wearer's resistance to enthralling and controlling spells and, in turn, strengthened the wearer's own such spells.

History
These amulets were given out to Valindra Shadowmantle's lieutenants as a sign of favor. Despite their empowering magics, Valindra could use the amulets to strangle those lieutenants who displeased her. After Valindra's defeat by the defenders of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR, some of these amulets were recovered from the villains' bodies.

## Amandarn

Amandarn was a rogue and adventurer in the mid–8 century DR.

Description
He considered himself sufficiently short, being slim, almost child-sized, and boyish. He was taller than a dwarf, however.

Personality
As an adventurer, he saw himself as more of a "wealth redistributor" than a thief.

Possessions
He carried a glowblade and wore at least one black glove. His adventuring group later came to jointly own a castle.

Relationships
He was part of a group of adventurers together with Nuressa, Iyriklaunavan, and Folossan.

History
On a warm day in Mirtul in the, the group Amandarn was with ran into a Netherese tomb. They spotted a wizened corpse floating up near the ceiling, and a pair of pillars in a large room; after Iyriklaunavan struck the corpse with a spell, bringing it down, the pillars sprouted eyes and attacked with hundreds of rays.

After managing to get away from the pillars of eyes, which the corpse attacked and destroyed, they were approached by the corpse, which asked them what year it was. Nuressa replied with the date, to which the corpse immediately teleported away. The team decided to flee the tomb and leave it unexplored.

Later, it turned out that Amandarn had gambled all their money away shortly after; Folossan nearly killed him for it. The two stole back some of their coin, got caught, and fled. After spending the evening under an altar to Mystra, they found a small fortune and a deed to a castle by morning, which they presented to the rest of the team. Iyriklaunavan believed them, but only because he magically verified their account of their misdeeds.

The liquor had a slightly bitter taste due to being made using rinds of small citrus fruits that resembled oranges. Amberfly was typically bottled in vessels of opaque green glass.

Amberfly was popular among the wealthy of Suzail in Cormyr, and merchants and traders on the Sword Coast, all along the Trade Way, and as far north as the city of Luskan.

## Amberside

Amberside was a large open market in Berdusk in the Western Heartlands in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
The market was located in the market quarter of Berdusk, just inside the city's northeast gate. The market was a maze of small tents and stalls of various colors, which were packed enough to be considered a good meeting spot for individuals who didn't want to be spotted by someone they knew.

Amberside was usually very busy, owing to the Berduskan traditions allowing its citizens to work quarter-shifts so that they could peruse the wares of the city's various shops.

Services
The market stalls in Amberside mostly sold small, useful, and non-perishable wares, setting it apart from the more established businesses in the city that sold larger goods. Due to the heavy traffic from merchants and peddlers through Berdusk, Amberside's stalls and wares changed frequently, sometimes selling rare or exotic goods from other regions. There was also a fair amount of household ironmongery, as a result of the place being named for a blacksmith named Ilm Amberal at the market's edge.

## Amberforge

The Amberforge was a smithy in the Central Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Amberforge was a one-story building in fair condition.

Description
The owner of the Amberforge, Amber Smithkin was known for her no-nonsense demeanor and an intolerance for disrespect, particularly in regard to her craft. Her reputation as a master of her trade, combined with her physically imposing presence, discouraged most from challenging her. However, customers who approached her with proper respect were rewarded with high-quality metal goods, including nails, horseshoes, pots, cauldrons, and various ironworking tools. The products she forged were durable and reliable, offered at average prices that reflect their solid workmanship.

Amber Smithkin owned and operated the Amberforge, formerly known as The Ringing Stone. Having inherited the business upon her father's death, Amber proved herself a skilled and dedicated blacksmith, carrying forward her family's legacy with pride. Despite having several brothers, it was Amber's passion and talent for ironworking that led her to take over the forge.

However, customers who approached her with proper respect were rewarded with high-quality metal goods, including nails, horseshoes, pots, cauldrons, and various ironworking tools. ## Amnesia

Amnesia was a mental condition that involved the loss of memory, which could be partial or complete. In certain circumstances, false memories were used to fill in the loss of memory.

Religion

Sufferers of mental illness frequently turned to the worship of Shar in the hopes of her curing their maladies. While some wished to have their lost memories restored, many more sought the oblivion of Shar as a means of relief from traumatic and painful memories. The darkcloaks of her church specialized in treating mental illness, though they often inflicted amnesia as a means of treating traumatic experiences.

Acquisition
The causes of amnesia could be physical, such as a head injury, psychological, such as a dissociative response to trauma, or magical influences like hostile psychic attacks. The intense study of arcane lore, particularly fell and foul arts, could also cause mental degradation that turned to amnesia.

For some extraordinary individuals, it could be part of their creation, resurrection, or reincarnation, or come from experiencing petrification, possession, cloning, and other shocks to the system.

The waters of the River Styx inflicted powerful memory loss upon those that touched it or drank from it, potentially erasing everything from them. This affected mortals, the souls of petitioners, and even planar beings. Only a few creatures like amnizu and the demon Crokek'toeck were immune to this.

Creatures & Plants
 Amnizu devils were immune to the Styx's effects, and their touch inflicted the same loss of memory upon their victims.
 Oblex slimes absorbed memories from victims, temporarily inflicting an amnesic state until they had a chance to recover.
 Obliviax was a peculiar plant that predated on sentient creatures and stole their memories.
 The rose of forgetfulness could completely obliterate the memory, personality, and learned skills of one who smelled it.
 Herald snakes had a venom that temporarily inflicted amnesia.
 Sapient undead like ghosts often suffered partial or complete loss of memory or personality upon their reanimation or reinstantiation, and it could worsen over time. In particular, liches could suffer extreme mental disorders including dementia and memory loss if their ritual of transformation went wrong, or if they were starved of their diet of souls.

Magic
 Brainkill destroyed a small part of the target's brain, causing an excruciating headache and physically erasing certain memories.
 Forget caused the loss of recent memories.
 Forget past caused recent memories to be lost.
 Memory rot physically attacked the target's brain with rotting spores, damaging it and its ability to retain and process information.
 Memory wrack temporarily severed the victim from their own long and short term memory, preventing them from recalling information or holding a thought for more than a few seconds.
 Modify memory could outright erase sections of someone's memory, in addition to altering existing memories or implanting new ones.
 Those abducted into the realm of Bluetspur were often victim to mental techniques that gave them amnesia or false memories.
 Alienists who delved too deep into maddening lore could encounter a concept so horrid that their mind intuitively defended itself, and hid away everything even tangentially related to the threat. This caused a kind of amnesia that blocked out their knowledge of arcana, the world in general, and their own life, leaving the sage completely unmoored with no context for their existence. Despite losing everything, they could eventually recover their lost memories, though this could conflict with whatever new personality and beliefs had emerged in the meantime.

Magical Items
 A potion of forgetfulness could erase a full day of recent memory.
 The Stone of Golorr connected to the user's mind in order to impart information, and if that connection ended it would attempt to wipe out all knowledge of its existence.
 A blade of forgetfulness caused victims to lose their recent memories, and prevented them from forming new ones for a few minutes.

Effects

Amnesia could vary widely in effect, ranging from missing the memories of specific events, time periods, people, or concepts, all the way to the complete loss of personality, acquired skills, and the basic facts of reality.

In mild cases, recurring amnesia could prevent the recall of minor facts and inconsequential events, creating an "absent-minded" appearance. In more extreme cases, it could continually block memories that were more than a few hours old.

Partial amnesia could leave the victim in a confused state where their memories and knowledge were self-obviously incomplete and did not have a coherent wholeness. Certain facts could be perfectly clear while others remained frustratingly blank. Skills and knowledge could be hidden to the person until their use was prompted, or lost entirely. In complete amnesia, the victim was utterly adrift in the world without any grounding.

Treatment
In some cases, amnesia was temporary and would eventually wear off once enough time had passed from the initial shock.

Medicine
 The desert's night flower, found in the realm of Set, could undo the amnesic effects of the Styx.
 Noblestalk was a mushroom that could be used to treat various ailments, including memory loss.

Magic
Various restorative spells could treat most forms of memory loss, depending on the severity and source. This included restoration, heal, limited wish, and wish.

 Psychic drain could cause amnesia in hosts if it drained enough psychic energy from them. Temporary and then permanent amnesia were quickly followed by permanent brain damage and then a coma.
 Remember helped restore lost memories, including from amnesia and possession, and could even provide insight from past lives.

Magic Items
 Forgetful robes aided the memory of the wearer, and were intended to assist those with reduced faculties.
 The necklace of memory enhancement gave the wearer immunity to any form of memory loss while wearing it, and gave them a limited form of eidetic memory.

Notable Affected
 Abdel Adrian suffered partial amnesia after the many tortures and experiments inflicted upon him by the wizard Jon Irenicus.
 Alias was a construct clone, and retained no memories of her creation and early existence. In addition, she had elaborate implanted memories that gave her the impression of having lived a full life.
 Baelen Bonecloak suffered brain damage from a lifetime of substance abuse, and slowly lost his ability to process memories.
 Billi Sharpe was cursed by a hag with both kleptomania and targeted amnesia regarding her brother.
 The Dark Urge suffered total memory loss and obliteration of personality from physical trauma, after Orin the Red stabbed him in the brain and implanted a mind flayer tadpole in the wound. It was possible for him to recover some memories after receiving magical healing.
 The mercenary adventurer Daud had amnesia from his early years, possibly from a head injury.
 The angel Demascus was slain many times in the course of his duties, and on each resurrection lost all memory of what had passed.
 The stone giant family of Gravillok, Speleosa, Rhodos, Obsidia, and Xortawere were cursed by Halaster to have a memory lasting only eight hours, keeping them trapped near Maddgoth's Castle in Undermountain.
 Ireena Kolyana of Barovia experienced amnesia as a child after a terrifying dire wolf attack, leading to her adoption by a new family without knowing her origins.
 Lyressa was a reanimated undead woman who had no awareness of her past life, despite her otherwise complete mental faculties.
 The famous Mordenkainen experienced complete memory loss following his battle with the vampire Strahd in Barovia. A combination of the psychological trauma of his failure and the horrors of the domain drove him to the state. Fortunately, his state was curable.
 The hero Minsc suffered a number of issues affecting his memory and reasoning that stemmed from his life of adventure. Most notably he suffered a gnoll-induced brain injury, and at one point he fell into the River Styx.
 The Nameless One experienced various levels of amnesia throughout his many deaths and resurrections, developing several new discrete personalities as he encountered the world each time. This culminated in his final incarnation, which suffered the complete loss of his personality, self-awareness, and the basic facts of reality. He was able to handle this somewhat by working with those that had previously met him, leaving messages and resources for his future self, and magically storing memories for later recovery.
 Palonya, a priest of Jergal, suffered a loss of memory after being afflicted with necromantic energies.
 Shadowheart had complete memory loss magically inflicted upon her by Viconia DeVir as a means of ensuring her loyalty and devotion, though her memories and core personality traits continued to re-emerge.
 Tristan Kendrick suffered temporary amnesia caused by the physical trauma of a sahuagin attack.
 The Wanderer was a mysterious warrior who lost his memory after the Battle of Kiroshima.

## Amalrae

Speaker Amalrae was a member of an intrigue in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Abilities
She could cast spells.

Activities
Amalrae was part of the nameless cabal headed by Halaster Blackcloak.

Relationships
She was part of a cell within the nameless cabal under Halaster, and this cell contacted him directly on at least one occasion. Her cell had eighteen people, fifteen of whom were Red Wizards, including Azmyrandyr and Roeblen,Sixteen people are at a table for that cabal's meeting, and it is known Azmyrandyr and Roeblen are part of the cabal, but absent. Thus, though thirteen people are described as wearing the purple and red robes of Thay, the habitual number of Red Wizards in that cabal is actually fifteen. and two of whom were Sharran priests.

Skloon was her rival, whom she hated.

In the, after the killing of the Red Wizards Azmyrandyr and Roeblen, Amalrae attended a meeting with the rest of their cabal; there, she learned that Thaltar Glaervar, a third Red Wizard linked to the other two by the magics of Halaster Blackcloak, had fought the Simbul and prevailed, taking a wand of hers as a trophy. Before more could be discussed, the wand proved to be the Simbul herself shapechanged; she soon turned herself into an energy field that eliminated all of them.

The amulet's powers came from the fey tear, it held fey's natural affinity to magic and their natural charm. These magics were only available for bards to channel, boosting their natural charisma, and offer boosted defense. Additionally, the amulet allowed bards to memorize one additional spell from the first, second, and third circles of magic.

Amulets of Songs were created by the dwarvish smith of Icewind Dale, Graendel Granitefist, sometime before the . Since then, such enchanted pieces of jewelry could be found on the necks of many bards, dazzling the stages of many inns and taverns across Faerûn, and inspiring their comrades on the fiends of battle.

## A Modest Guide to Sword Coast Wine

A Modest Guide to Sword Coast Wine was a sommelier book with a self-explanatory title, published sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Content
As it was evident from the title, the guide touched upon a variety of wines and sherries produces across the Sword Coast region of Faerûn. The book praised such vintages as Berduskan Dark and Arabellan Dry. It complimented Elverquisst's "sinuous fruity flavours," and favorably mentioned throat-slamming notes of Red Dragon Crush, notably not produced on the Sword coast but found there via adventurers and traders who dealt with the Vistani folk of the Domains of Dread. Thayan wines were praised for their red color and Saerloonian Glowfire for its pale hues. Lastly, the guide's author proclaimed that mulled or fortified wines, such as zzar and Daggerford clarry to be as suitable for drinking as bugbear's nether region.

History
In the, a copy of A Modest Guide to Sword Coast Wine could be found in the kitchen of The Singing Lute in Baldur's Gate's Lower City.

## Amanthan

Amanthan was a human wizard who resided in Arabel, Cormyr, in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Amanthan was described as a tall, young mage.

Personality
Amanthan was known to be reclusive, preferring to remain within his walled mansion in Arabel, away from the city's bustle. He was protective of his property and privacy, and was not afraid to defend himself with his magic.

According to Khelben Arunsun, Amanthan was "a good man" and "one of his more promising apprentices." The Blackstaff described him as being "too shy and kind to ever be a leader, or have much to do with power or politics." He was content to keep "behind his high walls and work on spells for as long as Faerûn lets him."

Residence
Amanthan lived in a walled mansion in Arabel, complete with gardens, fishponds with statues, and an ivy-covered wall that separated his property from neighboring estates.

History
Amanthan was once an apprentice of Khelben Arunsun, the Blackstaff of Waterdeep, and was considered one of his more promising students.

At some point, he relocated to Arabel in Cormyr, where he established himself as a reclusive wizard.

In the, Amanthan's home was used as a temporary refuge by the Swords of Eveningstar (later known as the Knights of Myth Drannor), who were fleeing from Purple Dragons pursuing them through the city. He allowed them to pass through his property and use a magical portal on his grounds that led to Waterdeep.

Following this encounter, Amanthan was temporarily possessed by the entity known as Old Ghost.

Relationships
Amanthan maintained a connection with his former mentor, Khelben, who spoke well of him. Through this connection, he was involved in some of Khelben's plans regarding the Swords of Eveningstar.

## Amaranth (leShay)

Lady Amaranth, sometimes styled as Amaranth leShay or known as the Rose of Sarifal and Yellow Rose of Sarifal, was a half-leShay princess of Sarifal in the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR. Prior to her return to Sarifal in the late 1470s DR, she had been the leader of a community of lycanthropes at Caer Moray on the island of Moray for a decade.

Description
She was legendarily beautiful, although in a somewhat mournful way. Her eyes were dark, her hair was red, and her skin was pale and clear with freckles. She had a yellow Rose of Sarifal tattoo on her neck: a sophisticated and intricate climbing rose, the royal symbol of the leShay.{{Cite digital book/The Rose of Sarifal/Kindle||4}

She was accustomed to dressing plainly and androgynously, or in leather armor when heading into danger.

Personality

Amaranth was kind-hearted and more than a little naïve about the world. She believed in helping those in need, and encouraged her followers to do the same, even when it was against their nature. She was prone to very simple, direct speech, and while she projected a demeanor of innocence, she was no stranger to combat. Being young, she had yet to display any of the laziness or imperiousness of her immortal kin, but still lacked any meaningful sense of urgency. She could be emotional and sentimental, and she craved interaction with sophisticated or artistic people.

Following a formative exchange with an apparent aspect of Araushnee, she finally realized what it meant to be immortal (or near-immortal) among mortals, and resolved to suppress her sentimentality and conduct herself in a more purposeful, perhaps manipulative fashion. It was at that time she resolved to become a queen and to avenge herself against those who had wronged her or tried to humiliate her.

Abilities
She had a powerful charisma that was perhaps supernatural. If she wanted, she could compel others to look at and admire her. However, the love others felt towards towards her could be out of her control, such as the case of her followers at Caer Moray being unwilling to let her leave them, and even going so far as to sabotage her efforts to go home to Sarifal.

She was an excellent archer and skilled with a short sword.

Relationships

Amaranth's parents were both killed when she was very young, although she could still recall conversations with her mother, Princess Callia of Sarifal. Her maternal family, the leShays of Sarifal, valued the purity of their bloodline in the extreme, and so the decision by her mother to have a child with an elf was seen as an affront. Amaranth's half-sister, the High Lady Ordalf of Sarifal, despised her and wished her dead. Her nephew, Prince Araithe, did not wish for this, and instead desired to have Amaranth bear him a child (as he considered her to be his most fertile potential mate for propagating a nearly-pure bloodline). As the youngest member of the leShay family, tradition dictated that she was the heir and successor to the royal line.

Her father had been a Llewyrr elf and member of the Knights of Synnoria. Her paternal uncle was Lord Mindarion, who aspired to elevate Amaranth to the throne of Sarifal by overthrowing High Lady Ordalf and Prince Araithe.

Her lycanthrope followers at Caer Moray were fiercely protective of her, and were marked with tattoos in the style of Amaranth's own yellow rose. Among these followers, she had been very close with the former matriarch, the werewolf Deucala, and had then become close to Deucala's offspring, notably Bay, Coal, and Lightfoot.{{Cite digital book/The Rose of Sarifal/Kindle||12} However, her most trusted friend was a wereboar named Poke.

She developed a budding romantic relationship with Lukas, the captain of the Sphinx, but ended this dalliance after internalizing that the gap between her as an immortal and him as a human meant that such a relationship would not only be transitory, but beneath her.

History
Early Life

Amaranth was born circa the late 1450s DR. Her nephew, Araithe, was so incensed that her mother (his grandmother) had chosen to have a child with an elf rather than with himself that he had both of Amaranth's parents killed. When she was seven, he brought her to live with him at Citadel Umbra, but by the time she was nine, she had been relocated to High Karador by High Lady Ordalf. Ordalf was pregnant at this time (with Araithe's child), but when her newborn son did not survive, it was known that she would try to have Amaranth killed. Two of Amaranth's caretakers, Mistresses Valeanne and Tiana, conspired to smuggle the young girl out of Sarifal with the help of Shamasar and his dragonborn soldiers. They stole out of Karador and rode to Crane Point, intending to fly the girl via hippogriff to the court of Erliza Daressin on Snowdown. However, the group was attacked by drow in service to the leShay, and while Amaranth was successfully handed off to a hippogriff rider, that rider was badly injured and soon succumbed to his wounds. The girl plummeted to the ground on the island of Moray, where she was badly injured. However, she was discovered and nursed back to health by a lycanthrope named Deucala, who led an offshoot of the Black Blood tribe. Although very young, Amaranth would soon come to be beloved and seen as the leader of this pack.

By the late 1470s DR, she was known on Moray as "Lady Amaranth" or "the Yellow Rose". Her followers within Caer Moray were predominantly female lycanthropes who strove to live as humanly as possible and to make and craft useful things. By her order, they had rebuilt the old castle for safety and had begun to share food with the nearby Northlander settlers, especially during lean times, in a version of the Feast of Stags. This drew the ire of the Malar-worshiping members of the Black Blood tribe, notably Argon Bael.

Return to Sarifal

By the time Amaranth was 19, she desperately wished to find a way home to Sarifal, feeling stifled living among the bestial lycanthropes and having been too young to have understood the machinations of her half-sister and nephew when she had left Sarifal a decade prior. However, most of her followers refused to allow her to leave, and so she tasked her special friend Poke with building a boat and travelling to Gwynneth across the Strait of the Leviathan to find Ordalf and request her help. Poke's mission resulted in her capture and eventual death,{{Cite digital book/The Rose of Sarifal/Kindle||13} and provided Ordalf with Amaranth's location, which she had sought for ten years. Ordalf promptly arranged for a group of adventurers—the crew of the Sphinx—to sail to Moray and to return with Amaranth's head.

The Sphinx crew became separated at Moray, and Amaranth would encounter two of them—Lukas and Gaspar-shen—when she saved them after they picked a fight with orcs to save some human captives. Intrigued by their apparent selfless behavior (which her upbringing suggested should be impossible for humans), she brought the injured warriors to Caer Moray to recuperate. Days later, the Black Blood tribe would rise up under the leadership of an aspect of Malar and lay siege to Caer Moray. During this battle, Amaranth and Lukas began to develop feelings, and shared a kiss. As the battle turned against the defenders, Amaranth was visited by a vision of Chauntea, who said that her worshipers wished for the young leShay to return to Sarifal and liberate it from its rulers.{{Cite digital book/The Rose of Sarifal/Kindle||10} Chauntea summoned a great wave to wash away both the attackers and the town's walls, leaving Amaranth little choice but to leave Caer Moray behind and follow the goddess's signs to a portal in the Breasal Marsh to Citadel Umbra. She made this journey with Lukas, Gaspar-shen, and her friend Coal, but Coal lost his life at the portal, rattling her.

Soon after arriving at Citadel Umbra, she met with Araithe, who invited her to stay with him. She was entranced at the thought of finally being back with the fey, and might have accepted his offer if Ordalf had not arrived and attempted to humiliate and perhaps kill her. In the ensuing confrontation that erupted between Araithe and Ordalf, Amaranth fled the citadel with Lukas and his allies. They were offered shelter by the drow servants of Araithe—the same ones who had tried to capture her when she was a child—who informed her that the Llewyrr of Synnoria would support her in overthrowing Ordalf, and asked that she agree to allow them to live on the surface if they aided her. She was not eager to accept this offer, and so the drow betrayed her, attempting to use her blood in a ritual to summon Lolth to the Material Plane.

Before they could be sacrificed, Amaranth and her companions were freed seemingly by an aspect of Araushnee, who possessed the drow priestess Amaka and killed the other drow. As the goddess departed, she whispered words of warning and of encouragement in the young leShay's ear, telling her to embrace her immortal heritage. With this advice, Amaranth found herself eager to pursue the throne of Sarifal and to discard her mortal allies, although this was at least in part because she knew that Lukas's best chance of survival was to flee Sarifal without her. After receiving a vision of herself dancing with her nephew, learning that the leShay army was approaching, and hearing of Poke's death, she resolved to parlay with Araithe to save Lukas and his friends, and to pursue her destiny of revenge against Ordalf and to rule as the future queen of Sarifal, whether it took a thousand years or a mere human lifetime.

## Amaka

Amaka was a handmaiden of Araushnee who presided over a shrine to her goddess beneath Citadel Umbra in the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
She was young and beautiful, and looked like a teenager to human sensibilities. She had wide red eyes, a wide mouth, heavy lips, and an arched nose. She wore her white hair cropped, and dressed in white linen tied with a red cord or fastened with a pin.

Personality
She was starved for the kind of intimate and caring relationships that lovers shared, and that she could not experience among the drow. She was also flirtatious, acting drunken or sensual in situations that did not call for it. She felt strongly that blood and violence were not appropriate ways to appeal to her goddess, and so abhorred her people's willingness to betray and kill those they worked with (and when forced to participate in such treachery, she was left weeping and unsteady with nerves). She seemed woefully naïve about the politics of the wider Moonshae Isles, and was breathlessly excited when speaking about her goddess.

Activities

Amaka and her sisters maintained a shrine in the tunnels beneath Citadel Umbra dedicated to Araushnee, which was unusual in its dense use of plants that made the air uncomfortably thick with oxygen. Her theology was confusing to outsiders: a heavy focus was placed on Aurushnee's innocence, including sexual innocence and a distaste for violence, and although she acknowledged Araushnee to be Lolth, she considered them to be two of up to four distinct aspects of her goddess. These four manifestations were: a beautiful elf, a spider, an elf/spider hybrid, and a hypersexualized human woman. She further believed that the guise of "the Spider Queen" was a curse inflicted upon Araushnee by Corellon, and that her goddess could be redeemed to her old self with the help of a greater deity, specifically Chauntea, who could "raise her out of the Abyss".

Relationships
Amaka's father was a warlock and the leader of a drow mercenary army who served Prince Araithe of Sarifal at Citadel Umbra during the 15 century DR. Her sisters and fellow handmaidens of Araushnee were Chinedu, Kemdelime, and Onyiye, and they in turn were subordinate to a hierophant who operated out of another shrine to the south of Citadel Umbra.

She was fearful of fomorians.

History
Amaka's clan came into the service of Prince Araithe of Sarifal after his eladrin retainers began to abandon him in the early 15 century DR. They were serving as his private army by the 1460s DR and were living beneath Citadel Umbra by the 1470s DR. This arrangement allowed for Amaka to build her shrine beneath the Citadel. At the same time, the clan's hierophant to the south tried desperately to summon Araushnee with all the tools and rituals at her disposal, but to no avail. At some point it was suggested that blood of the leShay royal family of Sarifal might be used to summon her, although Amaka rejected this idea.

In the late 1470s DR, Amaka and her sisters received visions foretelling of Lady Amaranth's arrival to their shrine via a portal. These visions, seemingly given by the Earthmother, led them to believe that the time of their goddess's return was joyously imminent. Amaka's father, knowing that the Llewyrr elves would be willing to follow Lady AMaranth to overthrow Prince Araithe and his mother, tried to play both sides in order to assure his clan's future. When Amaranth refused to acknowledge his primary demand—that the drow be allowed to live aboveground in the Winterglen and to build a true temple to Araushnee—he decided to trick her into travelling to the shrine of the clan's hierophant to be sacrificed in a ritual to summon the goddess from the Abyss.

Amaka was tasked with guiding Amaranth and her companions to the sacrificial site, although she did so against her will and the journey caused her much anguish. She believed not only that the plan would fail but that Araushnee would be displeased. Even so, she led the group into the trap, but then chose to fight alongside Amaranth when the hierophant's drow ambushed them.

She was next seen by Amaranth's party after they had been captured. She helped Lukas escape his bonds after telling him that she was jealous of the budding relationship between himself and Amaranth. She then whispered words of encouragement to Amaranth with a very different message, namely to embrace her heritage as an immortal leShay and reject connections to mortals like Lukas. It was then that she revealed herself to be more than a mortal drow, and instead an apparent avatar of Araushnee who had already killed the drow who had captured the party. She departed and allowed them to leave.The Rose of Sarifal does not clarify the nature of Amaka/Araushnee's final appearance. It can be interpreted as Amaka having been possessed by her goddess to free Amaranth or it may be inferred that Amaka died in the preceding fight and Araushnee simply assumed her appearance.

## Ambletrees Arms

Ambletrees Arms was an inn of Athalantar in the 3 century DR.

Location
Ambletrees Arms was on the northern side of the Delimbiyr River.Elmara moved northeast of Narthil and swam across a river. As Narthil is close to Mystra's Dance, this article assumes that the river in question is the Delimbiyr, and having to move north before crossing it would require her to be on the southern side.

Interior
The kitchen had stairs up and a hearth. There was a cellar in the building, and a cesspit somewhere on the property.

Services
There were drinkers, beds, and a kitchen; at least one traveler had her clothes cleaned and dried.

History
In Flamerule of the, Phaernos Bauldyn, the innkeeper, briefly hosted Elmara, a priestess of Mystra who was secretly the prince Elminster Aumar of Athalantar. She did not carry any coin, but paid her stay by magically increasing the room in the cellar and the cesspit. In exchange, she was stocked with provisions and her clothes were cleaned and dried.

## Amalzen

Amalzen was a guard captain of House Dun'Robar in Sschindylryn, who served at the estate during the invasion of Eveningstar in the 1480s DR.

Description
Amalzen wore a suit of plate mail and carried an enormous curved falchion.

History
Amalzen served as a senior officer of the house, but was unable to prevent the escape of Mirini Elemys to the surface. Dun'Robar was similarly unprepared for when she organized a vengeful raid on the estate to free the slaves and destroy the house's prestige, during which Amalzen was slain in combat.

## Amrennathed

Amrennathed was an amethyst dragon who was a great wyrm until the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
The great wyrm achieved an unlikely state of decrepitude by the end of her life.

Personality
An extremely proud individual, Amrennathed was quite fond of deception and intensely secretive.

Abilities
The dragon had abilities that altered the minds of others, as well as some scrying capabilities, of an unexplained nature. At the time of its death, it demonstrated a unique ability to merge with the mountain where it had ensconced itself, an act that may have eventually been the cause of the guardians that later inhabited its grave.

Possessions
She had some of a hoard left to her by the end of her life.

Activities
Amrennathed had devoted much energy and time to acquiring the secrets of others, even excising them from their memories to keep as her own.

Relationships
Secretly, Amrennathed kept in touch with a local old woman, Diadree, talking to her on occasion.

History
During her life, Amrennathed had stolen secrets from many people, including Iamras Sonmaire and one Zhentarim agent. While she was already a great wyrm, she shifted her lair to somewhere in the Mountains of the Alaoreum. In the, the great wyrm decided to pass on and so deny anyone the ability to target her mind and plumb it for secrets. To do this, she merged with the mountain in which she'd made her lair, creating earthquakes that damaged the nearby village of Orunn. However, her passing created a wave of mental or magical energy that placed some of her memories in a local woman, Diadree.

Rumors & Legends
A dragon known as the Queen of the Mountain was spoken of locally in Orunn, though in that usage, she was mostly a bogeyman to scare children with.

## Ambril's Bane

Ambril's Bane was a small battle between a handful of humans and dwarves in the 55 century before Dale Reckoning that caused the Shanatar Wars.

History
Around the year, a group of five Coramshite friends went tomb robbing on the north face of Mount Kellarak, adventurers and treasure hunters led by the heir of the bakkal of Coramshan. They found the burial site of a dwarven merchant's wife and sought to make off with three golden urns and a hammer, only to be caught by four dwarven miners who had no tolerance for their misdeeds. While the tomb was defended, the death of the heir caused the end of the good relations between Coramshan and Shanatar, leading to the millenia-long Shanatar Wars that ultimately destroyed the dwarven civilization and gave rise to the Calimshan Empire.

## Amazon (culture)

Amazon  was a term used to describe a human woman or organized groups of human women (sometimes by members of the group themselves) who practiced various forms of warfare and lived independently of men or exercised significant authority over them.

Description
While some suggested that women in the Realms were treated equally and could excel in any activity available to men, they nevertheless experienced varying degrees of derogation, discrimination, and restrictions imposed by men on how they could live because of their sex. As such, some human women were motivated to create communities far removed from men, where men occupied lower positions in the social hierarchy, or to individually pursue lifestyles that led them to distance themselves from men or exercise authority over them. These individuals and groups were called amazons.

Culture
Amazons, whether as groups or individuals, often regarded men as lesser beings and treated them with spite or discrimination, sometimes even enslaving them. Cultures, tribes, and countries considered amazonian often distanced themselves from men, keeping interactions with them to a minimum. Amazons often pursued a militaristic lifestyle or one connected to warfare.

At least some common culture among amazons existed, as evidenced by the existence of traditional amazonian songs.

Amazons were sometimes referenced in other cultures. The Crinti, the half-drow and half-elf aristocratic ruling class of Dambrath, were called the shadow amazons. The word amazon has sometimes been used as a descriptor for any tall, muscular or generally large woman of any raceGreg Marks (March 2010). Quench the Fire of the Raging God (MINI1-6). Living Forgotten Realms (RPGA), p. 13.Greg Marks (March 2010). Quench the Fire of the Raging God (MINI1-6). Living Forgotten Realms (RPGA), p. 16.Skip Williams, Jonathan Tweet, Monte Cook (July 2003). Monster Manual v.3.5. (Wizards of the Coast), p. 143. ISBN 0-7869-2893-X. in addition to women warriors in general.Wolfgang Baur (November 1993). Secrets of the Lamp. Genie Lore. (TSR, Inc.), p. 11. ISBN 978-1560766476.

Notable Amazons
Organizations
 The Jarnsaxa, a reclusive tribe, also known as "The Iron-Crushers of the World", that resided to the north of Yal Tengri, the Great Ice Sea of the Hordelands.
 The She-Wolves, a mercenary company based on the island of Ixinos in the Vilhon Reach.

Individuals
 Elissa Tarixon, the mysterious Queen who ruled the amazon isle of Ixinos.
 Neysea Snowwolf, a barbarian amazon from the Jarnsaxa tribe.
 Shinthala Deepcrest, a druidess who was called the Amazon Queen of Ilighôn.
 Vala, a hero and an adventuring fighter and member of the Silver Blades.

## Ambrythynn

Ambrythynn was a gnome inhabitant of Ieirithymbul in the late 14 century DR.

Relationships
Ambrythynn was an apprentice of Master Thintagast, alongside Burlmarr. To Burlmarr's knowledge, Ambrythynn fancied Maree Blimthaloon.

History
In Uktar of the, Ambrythynn and his fellow apprentice Burlmarr had been apprenticed to Master Thintagast since summer. One day, Burlmarr came in with a headache and thought he heard Ambrythynn claim he was going to propose to Lissa Boavartarr. Ambrythynn didn't quite follow.

Rumors & Legends
Burlmarr said he wouldn't tell Maree Blimthalloon about him fancying Lissa. This was not what was happening, as Ambrythynn wasn't talking.

## Amskar's army

Amskar's army was a force assembled by the Shadovar Archwizard Amskar in the late 15 century DR for the purpose of invading Cormyr. Created in parallel to the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon, it was intended to deliver a deathblow to the kingdom.

Organization

Amskar brought a force from Netheril with him, made up of priests of Shar, arcanists, shadar-kai, and shades. They served as officers and overseers of the wider force, making sure that the auxiliaries followed orders and were generally effective.

The vast bulk of the army was made up of local mercenaries and allies native to the Storm Horns region. This included orcs, gnolls, trolls, and harpies. Most notable of them were ten clans of hill giants, eight clans of stone giants and three clans of frost giants. They even attempted courting the white dracolich Arricaxerxes. Local humans were recruited to hunt and poach in the foothills, acting as a screening force and distraction from the real threat. Amskar was endlessly frustrated by the poor performance of the creatures he had tricked, threatened, and paid to serve him, and considered them completely disposable so long as his goals were met.

These auxiliaries took heavy losses as they performed labor and guarded the area, falling victim to wild beasts like owlbears, hungry dragons, being used to bait harpies, and being tricked by satyrs.

Base of Operations

The greatest concentration of forces were at the Netherese central camp, a fortified base in the snowy heights of the northwestern Storm Horns. Further into the mountain was Rungnir's glacier, in which an ancient Netherese mythallar was buried, and from which Amskar's enclave rose. The rest of the forces were scattered about in the peaks and foothills in temporary camps and fortifications.

History

Around the time of the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon overseen by Archwizard Tyvrus, Archwizard Amskar created his own operation hidden in the Storm Horns mountains. He set about making deals with local and regional groups, recruiting large numbers of orcs and gnolls as footsoldiers and even many giants. Most important of all were the frost giants represented by Rungnir, whose glacier home contained the mythallar the Netherese sought. This growing army was held together by a delicate web of threats and promises, and was constantly at risk of collapsing. Secrecy was of vital importance, and every effort was made to ensure that Cormyr wouldn't learn of the threat massing inside their borders. By coincidence this was greatly aided by the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, as the Purple Dragons and War Wizards were fully devoted to fighting the drow armies and couldn't be spared to investigate the Storm Horns. The ultimate goal of Amskar's army was for it to garrison Amskar's enclave and have it fly across Cormyr in a surprise attack upon Suzail. With the capital taken by surprise, Cormyr would surely fall or at least be vulnerable to further attacks, such as from Sembia and Wheloon.

This fell apart when Oriphaun Huntsilver arrived in the area, fresh from stealing the Nether Scroll in Wheloon Prison and evading Shadovar pursuit on the High Road. Acting as an agent of the Harpers, he pursued clues that led him into the foothills and then the peaks of the Storm Horns. Although he was eventually captured by Amskar and Shai at Yarrow Glade, the wider Harper network had been alerted, with Kara Patwin and Lorant Tass taking the lead on the investigation. Elsewhere, the hunters Olyce Tidewater and Thanbard Krys stumbled into an orcish patrol, barely surviving to report it in Eveningstar. Another major stumble for the Shadovar occurred almost simultaneously, when a group of Amskar's orcs attempted a raid upon Eveningstar but were defeated in detail. While the magistrates and many others were willing to dismiss it as a random tribal raid, the high-quality gear from Netheril they carried were a sign that greater forces were at play. This led Rina the Keen-Blade to send a raid upon the supply convoys in Tumblerock Pass and Orin Arroway to hire a strike upon the Netherese central camp high in the Storm Horns, inflicting great losses upon the no-longer-hidden army. Renton Finne followed this up by sending a group to find the frost giant Rungnir, who had grown suspicious and intolerant of the Netherese squatting upon his glacier. Though he was driving into hiding by his former allies, the Cormyreans managed to help him defeat Archwizard Tharno, causing his whole clan to join him in rebelling against the Netherese.

Despite the losses and setbacks, the activation of the mythallar succeeded and Amskar raised a crude Netherese enclave from the raw ice of the glacier. A final strike from the Harpers reached the glacier and rescued Oriphaun, only to find themselves aloft in the sky as the glacier flew south. They fought a desperate battle across the enclave and managed to critically damage its ability to fly while slaying Amskar, causing it to crash into a mountainside with the remaining army aboard it.
